4
Extension to Non-Oracle Case

Having analyzed the oracle case in the previous section,
we now extend the analysis to the non-oracle case, where
the value of
\mathbf{B}^{\natural}
is not given.
Different from the oracle case,
the ML estimator reduces to a

quadratic assignment problem
(QAP) as opposed to LAP.
As a mitigation, we adopt the estimator in
Zhang and Li 2020
,
which reconstructs the permutation matrix within the LAP framework, i.e.,

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}^{\mathsf{oracle}}

\displaystyle=\textup{argmin}_{\bm{\Pi}}\left\langle\bm{\Pi},-\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{Y}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\right\rangle,~\textup{~s.t.~}~~\sum_{i}\bm{\Pi}_{ij}=1,\sum_{j}\bm{\Pi}_{ij}=1,\bm{\Pi}\in\left\{0,1\right\}^{n\times n}.

(19)

We expect this estimator can yield good
insights of the permuted linear regression since

•

this estimator can reach the statistical optimality in a broad range of parameters;

•

this estimator exhibits a phase transition
phenomenon, which follows a similar pattern to that in the oracle case.

Following the same procedure as in Section
3
, we identify the
phase transition threshold
\mathsf{snr}
with Theorem
1
. To begin with, we write the random variable
\Xi
as

\displaystyle\Xi\cong\mathbf{Y}_{i}\mathbf{Y}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)^{\top},\vskip 2.84526pt

where
i
and
j
are uniformly distributed among the set

\left\{1,2,\cdots,n\right\}
. Afterwards, we adopt the Gaussian approximation scheme illustrated in Subsection
3.2
and
determine the phase transition points by first computing

\mathbb{E}\Xi
and
\textup{Var}\Xi
, respectively.

Theorem 2
.

For the random variable
\Xi
defined
in (
20
),
its mean
\mathbb{E}\Xi
and
variance
\textup{Var}\Xi
are

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi\simeq~

\displaystyle n\left(1-\tau_{h}\right)\left[\left(1+\tau_{p}\right){\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}+m\tau_{p}\sigma^{2}\right],

\displaystyle\textup{Var}\Xi\simeq~

\displaystyle n^{2}\tau_{h}\left(1-\tau_{h}\right)\tau_{p}^{2}\left[{\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}+m\sigma^{2}\right]^{2}+n^{2}\left[2\tau_{p}+3\left(1-\tau_{h}\right)^{2}\right]{\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle n^{2}\left[6\tau_{p}\left(1-\tau_{h}\right)^{2}+\left(3-\tau_{h}\right)\tau_{p}^{2}\right]{\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2},\vskip 2.84526pt

respectively,
where the definitions of

\tau_{p}
and
\tau_{h}
can be found
in Section
3
.

The proof of Theorem
2
is quite complicated, which is a combination of Wick’s theorem, Stein’s lemma, the conditional technique, and the leave-one-out technique, etc. For the presentation conciseness, we only give an outline of the proof and defer the technical details to Appendix.

4.1
Proof outline

To begin with, we decompose the random variable
\Xi
as

\displaystyle\Xi=~

\displaystyle\Xi_{1}+\sigma\left(\Xi_{2}+\Xi_{3}\right)+\sigma^{2}\Xi_{4},

(20)

where
\Xi_{i}

(1\leq i\leq 4)
are respectively defined as

\displaystyle\Xi_{1}\triangleq~

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\mathbf{B}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\bm{\Pi}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{X}(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}),

\displaystyle\Xi_{2}\triangleq~

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{X}(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}),

\displaystyle\Xi_{3}\triangleq~

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{B}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\bm{\Pi}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{X}(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}),

\displaystyle\Xi_{4}\triangleq~

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{X}(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}).\vskip 2.84526pt

Unlike the oracle case,
obtaining a closed-form expression of
\mathbb{E}e^{-\theta\Xi}
would be too difficult.
Hence, we adopt the Gaussian approximation method
as presented in Section
3.2
.
The task then transforms to computing the
expectation and variance of
\Xi
.

Computation of the mean
\mathbb{E}\Xi
.

For the computation of the mean
\mathbb{E}\Xi
, we can
verify that
\mathbb{E}\Xi_{2}
and
\mathbb{E}\Xi_{3}
are both zero, due to the independence between
\mathbf{X}
and
\mathbf{W}
.
For
\mathbb{E}\Xi_{1}
and
\mathbb{E}\Xi_{4}
,
we adopt Wick’s theorem
(
Janson 1997
)
to obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{1}=~

\displaystyle n\left(1-\tau_{h}\right)\left(1+\tau_{p}\right)\left[1+o_{\textup{P}}\left(1\right)\right]{\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{4}=~

\displaystyle nm\tau_{p}\left(1-\tau_{h}\right)[1+o_{\textup{P}}\left(1\right)].\vskip-5.69054pt\vskip 2.84526pt

Computation of the variance
\textup{Var}\Xi
.

Since

\textup{Var}\Xi=\mathbb{E}\Xi^{2}-(\mathbb{E}\Xi)^{2}
, we just need to
compute
\mathbb{E}\Xi^{2}
, which can be expanded into the following six terms

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi^{2}=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{1}^{2}+\sigma^{2}\mathbb{E}\Xi_{2}^{2}+\sigma^{2}\mathbb{E}\Xi_{3}^{2}+\sigma^{4}\mathbb{E}\Xi_{4}^{2}+2\sigma^{2}\mathbb{E}\Xi_{1}\Xi_{4}+2\sigma^{2}\mathbb{E}\Xi_{2}\Xi_{3}.\vskip-5.69054pt

(21)

The computation of the above terms turns out to be quite complex due to the
high-order Gaussian random variables. For example, the term
\mathbb{E}\Xi_{1}^{2}

involves the eighth-order Gaussian moments,
the terms
\mathbb{E}\Xi_{2}^{2},\mathbb{E}\Xi_{3}^{2},\mathbb{E}\Xi_{1}\Xi_{4}
and

\mathbb{E}\Xi_{2}\Xi_{3}
all involve the sixth-order Gaussian variables, etc.
To handle the difficulties in computing
\mathbb{E}\Xi^{2}
,
we propose the following computation procedure, which
can be roughly divided into
3
phases.

•

Phase I: Leave-one-out decomposition.

The major technical difficulty comes from the
correlation between the product
\mathbf{X}^{\top}\bm{\Pi}^{\natural}\mathbf{X}
and
the difference
\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}
.
We decouple this correlation by first
rewriting the matrix

\mathbf{X}^{\top}\bm{\Pi}^{\natural}\mathbf{X}
as the sum

\sum_{\ell}\mathbf{X}_{\ell}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(\ell)}^{\top}
.
Then we collect all terms
\mathbf{X}_{\ell}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(\ell)}^{\top}

independent of
\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}
and
\mathbf{X}_{j}
in
the matrix
\bm{\Sigma}
and leave the remaining terms to the
matrix

\bm{\Delta}
, i.e.,
\bm{\Delta}\triangleq\mathbf{X}^{\top}\bm{\Pi}^{\natural}\mathbf{X}-\bm{\Sigma}
.
This decomposition shares the same
spirit as the leave-one-out technique
(
Karoui 2013
;
Bai and Silverstein 2010
;
Karoui 2018
;
Sur et al. 2019
)
. Then, we divide all terms in

\mathbb{E}\Xi^{2}
into
3
categories:

1)
terms only containing
matrix
\bm{\Sigma}
;
2)
terms containing both

\bm{\Sigma}
and
\bm{\Delta}
; and
3)
terms only containing
\bm{\Delta}
.

•

Phase II: Conditional technique.

Concerning the terms in the first two categories, which covers the
majority of terms,
we can exploit the independence among the rows in the sensing matrix

\mathbf{X}
. With the conditional technique, we can
reduce the order of Gaussian moments by
separately taking the expectation w.r.t
\bm{\Sigma}
and
w.r.t vectors
\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}
and
\mathbf{X}_{j}
.

•

Phase III: Direct computation.

For the few terms in the third category (i.e., terms only containing
\bm{\Delta}
),
we compute the high-order Gaussian moments by exhausting
all terms and iterative applying
of Wick’s Theorem and Stein’s Lemma, which
can reduce the higher-order Gaussian moments to lower-order Gaussian moments.

Adopting the above proof strategy, we obtain the following results for each term listing as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{1}^{2}\approx~

\displaystyle\left(n-h\right)^{2}\left(1+\frac{2p}{n}+\frac{p^{2}}{n(n-h)}\right)\left[\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\right]^{2}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle n^{2}\left[\frac{2p}{n}+3\left(1-\frac{h}{n}\right)^{2}+\frac{6(n-h)^{2}p}{n^{3}}+\frac{(3n-h)p^{2}}{n^{3}}\right]\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{2}^{2}\approx~

\displaystyle 2np\left(1+p/n\right)\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{3}^{2}\approx~

\displaystyle 2n^{2}\left(\frac{p}{n}+\left(1-\frac{h}{n}\right)^{2}+\frac{p^{2}}{n^{2}}+\frac{4p(n-h)^{2}}{n^{3}}\right)\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{4}^{2}\approx~

\displaystyle\frac{(n-h)m^{2}p^{2}}{n},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{1}\Xi_{4}\approx~

\displaystyle\frac{mp(n-h)(n+p-h)}{n}\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{2}\Xi_{3}\approx~

\displaystyle\ \frac{p(n-h)(n+p-h)}{n}\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}).\vskip 2.84526pt

Plugging the calculation results thereof to (
21
) and exploiting the relation
\textup{Var}\Xi=\mathbb{E}\Xi^{2}-(\mathbb{E}\Xi)^{2}
, we complete the proof
of Theorem
2
.

4.2
An illustrating example

This subsection predicts the phase transition points with Theorem
2
.
Unlike the oracle case,
we notice the edge weights
\mathbf{E}_{ij}
are
strongly correlated, especially when

j=\pi^{\natural}(j)
, which
corresponds to the non-permuted rows. To factor
out these dependencies, we only take the permuted rows
into account and correct the sample size from
n
to

\tau_{h}n
. The prediction
\mathsf{snr}_{\textup{non-oracle}}
is then computed by solving
2\log(n\tau_{h})\textup{Var}\Xi=\left(\mathbb{E}\Xi\right)^{2}
, where
\mathbb{E}\Xi
and

\textup{Var}\Xi
are in Theorem
2
.

To illustrate the prediction accuracy, we consider the case where
\mathbf{B}^{\natural}
’s singular values
are of the same order, i.e.,
\frac{\lambda_{i}(\mathbf{B}^{\natural})}{\lambda_{j}(\mathbf{B}^{\natural})}=O(1),~1\leq i,j\leq m
, where
\lambda_{i}(\cdot)
denotes the
i
-th singular value.
Then, we obtain the
\mathsf{snr}_{\textup{non-oracle}}
, which is written as

\displaystyle\mathsf{snr}_{\textup{non-oracle}}\approx{\eta_{1}}/{\eta_{2}}.

(22)

Here,
\eta_{1}
and
\eta_{2}
are defined as

\displaystyle\eta_{1}\triangleq~

\displaystyle 2\tau_{h}\tau_{p}^{2}\log\left(n\tau_{h}\right)-\tau_{p}(\tau_{p}+1)\left(1-\tau_{h}\right)+\tau_{p}\sqrt{2(1-\tau_{h})\tau_{h}\cdot\log\left(n\tau_{h}\right)},

\displaystyle\eta_{2}\triangleq~

\displaystyle\left(1-\tau_{h}\right)\left(\tau_{p}+1\right){}^{2}-2\tau_{h}\tau_{p}^{2}\log(n\tau_{h}).\vskip 2.84526pt

Note that the predicted
\mathsf{snr}_{\textup{non-oracle}}
varies for different
\tau_{h}
and
\tau_{p}
.
Viewing
\mathsf{snr}_{\textup{non-oracle}}
as a function of

\tau_{h}
, we observe a singularity point of

\tau_{h}
, i.e.,
\mathsf{snr}_{\textup{non-oracle}}(\tau_{h})=\infty
, or equivalently,
\eta_{2}(\tau_{h})=0
. This suggests a potential phase transition
phenomenon w.r.t.
\tau_{h}
.
This predicted phenomenon is confirmed by the numerical experiments, in which we vary the proportion of the permuted rows and study the change in the reconstruction error rate.

Remark 4
.

To isolate the reconstruction performance from the impact of
\mathsf{snr}
, we adopt the noiseless setting,
which corresponds to infinite
\mathsf{snr}
. Hence, the change in the error rate comes solely from the increasing number of permuted rows rather than the insufficient
\mathsf{snr}
.

Remark 5
.

The capability to predict the precise phase transition point of
\tau_{h}
is a novel feature of our method. In contrast, previous proof in
Zhang and Li 2020
only establishes
\tau_{h}
as being of the order
O(1)
, without specifying its exact values, which our method can now predict.

Figure 2:
Left panel
:
Predicted phase transition points
\mathsf{snr}_{\textup{non-oralce}}
.

Right panel
: Plot of the recovery rate under the noiseless
setting, i.e.,
\mathsf{snr}=\infty
.
Gaussian
:
\mathbf{B}^{\natural}_{ij}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textup{i.i.d}}}{{\sim}}\mathsf{N}(0,1)
;
Identity
:
\mathbf{B}^{\natural}=\mathbf{I}_{p\times p}
;

Block-diagonal
:
\mathbf{B}^{\natural}=\textup{diag}\left\{1,\cdots,1,0.5,\cdots,0.5\right\}
. We observe that the
correct recovery rates drop sharply within the
regions of our predicted value.

4.2.1
Impact of
n
on the phase transition point

We study the impact of
n
on
\tau_{h}
.
The numerical experiment
is shown in Figure
2
, where we study the dependence of

\mathsf{snr}_{\textup{non-oralce}}
on
\tau_{h}
.
We can see the predicted phase transition
\tau_{h}
matches
to a good extent to the numerical experiments.
Then, we fix the
p
and study the impact of
n
on
\tau_{h}
.
We observe that the phase transition
\tau_{h}
increases together with the
sample number
n
, which is also captured by our formula in
(
22
).

4.2.2
Limits of
\tau_{h}

In addition, we consider the limiting behavior of
\tau_{h}
when
\tau_{p}
approaches
0
, or equivalently,

p=o_{\textup{P}}\left(n\right)
. We can simplify
\mathbb{E}\Xi
and
\textup{Var}\Xi
in
Theorem
2
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi\simeq~

\displaystyle n\left(1-\tau_{h}\right){\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2},

\displaystyle\textup{Var}\Xi\simeq~

\displaystyle 3n^{2}\left(1-\tau_{h}\right)^{2}{\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}.\vskip 2.84526pt

We notice
that the singularity point in (
22
) disappears. In other words,
we can have the correct permutation matrix
\bm{\Pi}^{\natural}

even when
h\approx n
. This is (partly) verified by
Figure
3
, from which we observe that
the phase transition point w.r.t.
\tau_{h}
approaches to one, or equivalently,
h
approaches
n
,
as
\tau_{p}
decreases to zero.

Figure 3:
Plot of correct recovery rate w.r.t.
\tau_{h}
.
We consider the noiseless scenario (i.e.,
\mathsf{snr}=\infty
) and
pick
n=\{800,1000,1200,1400\}
.

5
Conclusion

The shuffled (permuted) regression problem is a well-known challenging task, with practical applications in databases, machine learning, and privacy. This is the first work that can
identify the precise location of
phase transition thresholds of permuted
linear regressions.
For the oracle case
where the signal
\mathbf{B}^{\natural}
is given as a prior, our analysis can predict the
phase transition threshold
\mathsf{snr}_{\mathsf{oracle}}
to a good extent.
For the non-oracle case
where
\mathbf{B}^{\natural}
is not given, we have
modified the leave-one-out technique
to approximately compute the phase critical
\mathsf{snr}_{\textup{non-oracle}}
value
for the phase transition, as the precise computation becomes significantly complicated
as the high-order interaction between Gaussian random variables is involved.
Moreover, we have associated the singularity point in
\mathsf{snr}_{\textup{non-oracle}}
with
a phase transition point w.r.t the maximum allowed number of permuted rows. Finally, we have presented many numerical experiments to corroborate the accuracy of our theoretical predictions.

Appendix A
Numerical Methods for the Phase Transition Points

In this section, we present the numerical method to compute the phase transition points. Notice that the correct recovery rate is in monotonic non-decreasing relation with the
\mathsf{snr}
, we adopt a binary-search-based method.

First, we fix the
\mathsf{snr}
and run the experiments for

100
times. Then, we calculate the error rate of permutation recovery (full permutation recovery). If the error rate is below
0.05
, we regard the corresponding
\mathsf{snr}
as above the phase transition point and try a smaller value. Otherwise, we regard the
\mathsf{snr}
as below the phase transition point and try a larger value. The detailed description is given in Algorithm
1
.

In our numerical experiments, we run
20
times of Algorithm
1
for each parameter setting. Then, we estimate its mean and the standard deviation from these estimated phase transition points.

1:

Initialization.
Set the initial search range for
\mathsf{snr}
as
[l,r]
.
Define the precision threshold
\varepsilon
.

2:

3:

while

\left\lvert l-r\right\rvert>\varepsilon

do

4:

Set
\mathsf{snr}_{\textup{middle}}=\frac{l+r}{2}
.

5:

Run experiments
100
times for this
\mathsf{snr}_{\textup{middle}}
.

6:

Compute the error rate of full permutation recovery.

7:

8:

if
the error rate is below
0.05

then

9:

\mathsf{snr}_{\textup{middle}}\rightarrow r-\varepsilon
, # we have
\mathsf{snr}_{\textup{middle}}
be greater than the phase transition point

10:

else

11:

\mathsf{snr}_{\textup{middle}}\rightarrow l+\varepsilon
. # we have
\mathsf{snr}_{\textup{middle}}
be no greater than the phase transition point

12:

end

if

13:

14:

end

while

15:

16:

Output.
Return the phase transition point
\mathsf{snr}_{\textup{middle}}
.

Algorithm 1

Numerical method to compute the phase transition points.

Complexity analysis
. For a given precision threshold
\varepsilon
, each iteration (Line
3
to Line
14
in Algorithm
1
) takes
O(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon})
rounds to converge and it runs the permutation recovery algorithm
100
times in each round. Hence, we run
20\times 100\times\log_{2}(10^{4})~(\approx 26575)
permutation recovery experiments for each parameter.

Appendix B
Analysis of the Non-Oracle Case

This section presents the technical details
in analyzing the non-oracle case, to put more specifically, Theorem
2
.

B.1
Notations

Note that our analysis can involve the terms
containing
\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}}(i)-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)

and
\mathbf{X}^{\top}\bm{\Pi}^{\natural}\mathbf{X}
simultaneously.
To decouple the dependence between

\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}}(i)-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)
and

\mathbf{X}^{\top}\bm{\Pi}^{\natural}\mathbf{X}
, we first rewrite the matrix

\mathbf{X}^{\top}\bm{\Pi}^{\natural}\mathbf{X}
as the sum

\sum_{\ell}\mathbf{X}_{\ell}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(\ell)}^{\top}

and then collect all terms
\mathbf{X}_{\ell}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(\ell)}^{\top}

independent of
\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}
and
\mathbf{X}_{j}
in
the matrix
\bm{\Sigma}
, which is written as

\displaystyle\bm{\Sigma}\triangleq\sum_{\ell,\pi^{\natural}(\ell)\neq\pi^{\natural}(i),j}\mathbf{X}_{\ell}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(\ell)}^{\top}.

(23)

The rest terms are then put in the matrix
\bm{\Delta}
such that

\mathbf{X}^{\top}\bm{\Pi}^{\natural}\mathbf{X}=\bm{\Sigma}+\bm{\Delta}
. Note that
the expression of
\bm{\Delta}
varies under different cases such that

•

Case
(s,s)
:
i=\pi^{\natural}(i)
and
j=\pi^{\natural}(j)
.
We have

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}=\bm{\Delta}^{(s,s)}=\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}+\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}.

(24)

•

Case
(s,d)
:
i=\pi^{\natural}(i)
and
j\neq\pi^{\natural}(j)
.
We have

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}=\bm{\Delta}^{(s,d)}=\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}+\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(j)}^{\top}+\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural-1}(j)}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}.

(25)

•

Case
(d,s)
:
i\neq\pi^{\natural}(i)
and
j=\pi^{\natural}(j)
.
We have

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}=\bm{\Delta}^{(d,s)}=\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}+\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}^{\top}+\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}.

(26)

•

Case
(d,d)
:
i\neq\pi^{\natural}(i)
and
j\neq\pi^{\natural}(j)
.
We have

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}=\bm{\Delta}^{(d,d)}=\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}+\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}^{\top}+\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(j)}^{\top}+\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural-1}(j)}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}.

(27)

In addition, we define the matrix
\mathbf{M}
as
\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\mathbf{B}^{\natural\top}
,
and define the index sets
{\cal S},{\cal D}
, and
{\cal D}_{\textup{pair}}
as

\displaystyle{\cal S}\triangleq

\displaystyle\left\{\ell~|~\ell\neq i~\textup{or}~j,~\ell=\pi^{\natural}(\ell)\right\},

(28)

\displaystyle{\cal D}\triangleq

\displaystyle\left\{\ell~|~\ell,\pi^{\natural}(\ell)\neq i~\textup{or}~j,~\ell\neq\pi^{\natural}(\ell)\right\},

(29)

\displaystyle{\cal D}_{\textup{pair}}\triangleq

\displaystyle\left\{(\ell_{1},\ell_{2}):\ell_{1}=\pi^{\natural}(\ell_{2}),\ell_{2}=\pi^{\natural}(\ell 1),\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\in{\cal D}\right\},

(30)

respectively.

B.2
Main Computation

First, we decompose
\Xi
as

\displaystyle\Xi=~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\mathbf{B}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\bm{\Pi}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{X}\left[\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right]}_{\triangleq\Xi_{1}}+\sigma\underbrace{\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\left[\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right]}_{\triangleq\Xi_{2}}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\sigma\underbrace{\mathbf{W}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{B}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\bm{\Pi}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{X}\left[\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right]}_{\triangleq\Xi_{3}}+\sigma^{2}\underbrace{\mathbf{W}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\left[\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right]}_{\triangleq\Xi_{4}}.\vskip 2.84526pt

The following context separately computes its expectation

\mathbb{E}\Xi
and its variance
\textup{Var}\Xi
.

Expectation.

We can easily verify that both
\mathbb{E}\Xi_{2}
and
\mathbb{E}\Xi_{3}
are zero.
Then our goal turns to calculating the expectation of
\mathbb{E}\Xi_{1}
and

\mathbb{E}\Xi_{4}
.
First, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{1}=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sum_{\ell=\pi^{\natural}(\ell)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\ell}\mathbf{X}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbb{E}\sum_{\ell}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(\ell)}\mathbf{X}_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}.\vskip 2.84526pt

With Lemma
15
and Lemma
16
, we conclude

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{1}=~

\displaystyle(n-h)\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})+(p+1)\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{i=\pi^{\natural}(i)}\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})-\left(p\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{i=j}+\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{j=\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}\right)\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle(n+p-h-hp/n)\left[1+o(1)\right]\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}).

(31)

Meanwhile, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{4}=~

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{W}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{1}~\cdots~\mathbf{W}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{i}~\cdots\mathbf{W}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{n}\right]\mathbf{X}\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle m\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)=mp\left(\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{i=\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{i=j}\right)=\frac{mp(n-h)\sigma^{2}}{n}\left[1+o(1)\right].

(32)

Combining (
B.2
) and (
B.2
) and neglecting the
o(1)
terms yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi\approx~

\displaystyle(n+p)\left(1-h/n\right){\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}+\frac{mp(n-h)\sigma^{2}}{n}.\vskip 2.84526pt

Variance.

Then we study the variance of
\Xi
. With the relation

\textup{Var}(\Xi)=\mathbb{E}\Xi^{2}-(\mathbb{E}\Xi)^{2}
, our goal reduces
to computing
\mathbb{E}\Xi^{2}
, which can be written as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi^{2}=\mathbb{E}\Xi_{1}^{2}+\sigma^{2}\mathbb{E}\Xi_{2}^{2}+\sigma^{2}\mathbb{E}\Xi_{3}^{2}+\sigma^{4}\mathbb{E}\Xi_{4}^{2}+2\sigma^{2}\mathbb{E}\Xi_{1}\Xi_{4}+2\sigma^{2}\mathbb{E}\Xi_{2}\Xi_{3}.\vskip 2.84526pt

The following context separately computes each term as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{1}^{2}\approx~

\displaystyle\left(n-h\right)^{2}\left(1+\frac{2p}{n}+\frac{p^{2}}{n(n-h)}\right)\left[\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\right]^{2}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle n^{2}\left[\frac{2p}{n}+3\left(1-\frac{h}{n}\right)^{2}+\frac{6(n-h)^{2}p}{n^{3}}+\frac{(3n-h)p^{2}}{n^{3}}\right]\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{2}^{2}\approx~

\displaystyle 2np\left(1+p/n\right)\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{3}^{2}\approx~

\displaystyle 2n^{2}\left(\frac{p}{n}+\left(1-\frac{h}{n}\right)^{2}+\frac{p^{2}}{n^{2}}+\frac{4p(n-h)^{2}}{n^{3}}\right)\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{4}^{2}\approx~

\displaystyle\frac{(n-h)m^{2}p^{2}}{n},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{1}\Xi_{4}\approx~

\displaystyle\frac{mp(n-h)(n+p-h)}{n}\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{2}\Xi_{3}\approx~

\displaystyle\ \frac{p(n-h)(n+p-h)}{n}\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}).\vskip 2.84526pt

The detailed computation is attached as follows.

Lemma 1
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{1}^{2}=~

\displaystyle\left(n-h\right)^{2}\left(1+\frac{2p}{n}+\frac{p^{2}}{n(n-h)}+o(1)\right)\left[\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\right]^{2}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle n^{2}\left[\frac{2p}{n}+3\left(1-\frac{h}{n}\right)^{2}+\frac{6(n-h)^{2}p}{n^{3}}+\frac{(3n-h)p^{2}}{n^{3}}+o(1)\right]\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}),\vskip 2.84526pt

where
\Xi_{1}
is defined in (
20
).

Proof.

We begin the proof by decomposing
\Xi_{1}^{2}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{1}^{2}=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\underbrace{\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Sigma}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)}_{\Lambda_{1}}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}\underbrace{\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)}_{\Lambda_{2}}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\underbrace{\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)^{\top}\bm{\Delta}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)}_{\Lambda_{3}},\vskip 2.84526pt

and separately bound each term as in
Lemma
2
, Lemma
3
,
and Lemma
4
.

∎

Lemma 2
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Sigma}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\left(n-h\right)^{2}\left(1+o(1)\right)\left[\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\right]^{2}+\ n^{2}\left[\frac{2p}{n}+3\left(1-\frac{h}{n}\right)^{2}+o(1)\right]\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}).\vskip 2.84526pt

Proof.

Due to the independence among different rows of the sensing matrix

\mathbf{X}
, we condition on
\bm{\Sigma}
and take expectation w.r.t.

\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}
and
\mathbf{X}_{j}
, which leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{1}=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\underbrace{\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Sigma}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{\Lambda_{1,1}}+\mathbb{E}\underbrace{\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Sigma}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}}_{\Lambda_{1,2}}.\vskip 2.84526pt

For
\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{1,1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{1,1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\small{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\textcircled{\raisebox{-.8pt}{1}}}}}}{{=}}~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\operatorname{Tr}\left(\bm{\Sigma}\mathbf{M}\right)\operatorname{Tr}\left(\bm{\Sigma}\mathbf{M}\right)\right]+\mathbb{E}\operatorname{Tr}\left(\bm{\Sigma}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{\top}\right)+\mathbb{E}\operatorname{Tr}\left(\bm{\Sigma}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbf{M}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\small{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\textcircled{\raisebox{-.8pt}{2}}}}}}{{=}}~

\displaystyle\left(n-h\right)^{2}\left[1+o(1)\right]\left[\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\right]^{2}+\ n^{2}\left[\frac{p}{n}+2\left(1-\frac{h}{n}\right)^{2}+o(1)\right]\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}),\vskip 2.84526pt

where
\raisebox{-.8pt}{1}⃝
is due to (
66
),
and
\raisebox{-.8pt}{2}⃝
is due to Lemma
12
, Lemma
13
, and Lemma
14
.
As for
\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{1,2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{1,2}=\mathbb{E}\operatorname{Tr}\left(\bm{\Sigma}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}^{\top}\right)=n^{2}\left[\frac{p}{n}+\left(1-\frac{h}{n}\right)^{2}+o(1)\right]\operatorname{Tr}\left(\mathbf{M}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\right),\vskip 2.84526pt

and hence complete the proof.
∎

Lemma 3
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)\approx\frac{(n-h)^{2}p}{n}\left[\left(\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\right)^{2}+3\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M})\right].\vskip 2.84526pt

Proof.

Similar as above, we first expand
\Lambda_{2}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2}=~

\displaystyle(n-h)\mathbb{E}\underbrace{\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{\Lambda_{2,1}}+(n-h)\mathbb{E}\underbrace{\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}}_{\Lambda_{2,2}}

\displaystyle-~

\displaystyle(n-h)\mathbb{E}\underbrace{\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}}_{\Lambda_{2,3}}-(n-h)\mathbb{E}\underbrace{\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{\Lambda_{2,4}}.\vskip 2.84526pt

Case
(s,s)
:
i=\pi^{\natural}(i)
and
j=\pi^{\natural}(j)
.

We first compute
\Lambda_{2,1}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,1}=~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}}_{\mathbb{E}{\left\|\mathbf{X}_{i}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)^{2}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}}_{\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\left(p+5\right)\left[\left(\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\right)^{2}+\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M})+\operatorname{Tr}\left(\mathbf{M}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\right)\right].\vskip 2.84526pt

We consider
\Lambda_{2,2}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,2}=~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)}_{\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)^{2}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)}_{\mathbb{E}{\left\|\mathbf{X}_{j}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\left(\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\right)^{2}+\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M})+\operatorname{Tr}\left(\mathbf{M}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\right)+(p+2)\operatorname{Tr}\left(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}\right).\vskip 2.84526pt

As for
\Lambda_{2,3}
and
\Lambda_{2,4}
, we can verify that
they are both zero, which gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2}=~

\displaystyle(n-h)p\left[1+o(1)\right]\left[\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\right]^{2}+3(n-h)p\left[1+o(1)\right]\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}).

(33)

Case
(s,d)
:
i=\pi^{\natural}(i)
and
j\neq\pi^{\natural}(j)
.
We can compute
\Lambda_{2,1}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,1}=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(j)}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{0}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural-1}(j)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{0}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\left(p+4\right)\left[\left(\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\right)^{2}+\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M})+\operatorname{Tr}\left(\mathbf{M}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\right)\right].\vskip 2.84526pt

We consider
\Lambda_{2,2}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,2}=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(j)}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}}_{0}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural-1}(j)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}}_{0}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)^{2}=\left(\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\right)^{2}+\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M})+\operatorname{Tr}\left(\mathbf{M}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\right).\vskip 2.84526pt

Similarly, we can verify that both

\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,3}
and
\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,4}
are
zero and hence have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2}=~

\displaystyle(n-h)p\left[1+o(1)\right]\left[\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\right]^{2}+2(n-h)p\left[1+o(1)\right]\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}).

(34)

Case
(d,s)
:
i\neq\pi^{\natural}(i)
and
j=\pi^{\natural}(j)
.

We compute
\Lambda_{2,1}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,1}=~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{0}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{0}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}=\left(\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\right)^{2}+\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M})+\operatorname{Tr}\left(\mathbf{M}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\right).\vskip 2.84526pt

We consider
\Lambda_{2,2}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,2}=~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}}_{0}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}}_{0}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}}_{\mathbb{E}{\left\|\mathbf{X}_{j}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}}=(p+2)\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}).\vskip 2.84526pt

As for
\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,3}
and
\Lambda_{2,4}
,
we can follow the same strategy and prove they are both
zero, which yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2}=~

\displaystyle(n-h)\left[\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\right]^{2}+(n-h)p\left[1+o(1)\right]\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}).

(35)

Case
(d,d)
:
i\neq\pi^{\natural}(i)
and
j\neq\pi^{\natural}(j)
.

Contrary to the previous cases, we have

\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,1}
and
\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,2}
to be
zero in this case rather than
\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,3}
and
\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,4}
.

Hence our focus turns to the calculation of

\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,3}
and that of
\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,4}
.
For
\Lambda_{2,3}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,3}=~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}}_{p\mathbbm{1}_{i=j}\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}}_{\mathbbm{1}_{j=\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(j)}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}}_{p\mathbbm{1}_{i=j}\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural-1}(j)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}}_{\mathbbm{1}_{j=\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle 2\left(p\mathbbm{1}_{i=j}+\mathbbm{1}_{j=\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}\right)\left[\left(\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\right)^{2}+\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M})+\operatorname{Tr}\left(\mathbf{M}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\right)\right].\vskip 2.84526pt

Then we turn to the calculation of
\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,4}
, which proceeds as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,4}=~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}{\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{\mathbbm{1}(i=j)\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{\mathbbm{1}_{j=\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}\mathbb{E}{\left\|\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(j)}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{\mathbbm{1}(i=j)\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural-1}(j)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{\mathbbm{1}_{j=\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}\mathbb{E}{\left\|\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle 2\mathbbm{1}_{j=\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}(p+2)\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M})+2\mathbbm{1}(i=j)\left[\left(\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\right)^{2}+\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M})+\operatorname{Tr}\left(\mathbf{M}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\right)\right].\vskip 2.84526pt

Then we conclude

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2}=-~

\displaystyle 2(n-h)\left[(p+1)\mathbbm{1}_{i=j}+\mathbbm{1}_{j=\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}\right]\left[\left(\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\right)^{2}+\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M})+\operatorname{Tr}\left(\mathbf{M}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\right)\right]

\displaystyle-~

\displaystyle 2(n-h)(p+2)\mathbbm{1}_{j=\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}).

(36)

The proof is thus completed by combining
(
33
),
(
34
), (
35
),
and (
B.2
).

∎

Lemma 4
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)^{\top}\bm{\Delta}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{h}{n}+o(1)\right)p^{2}\left[\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\right]^{2}+\left(3-\frac{h}{n}+o(1)\right)p^{2}\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}).\vskip 2.84526pt

Proof.

We begin the proof by expanding
\Lambda_{3}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{3}=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\underbrace{\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{\Lambda_{3,1}}+\mathbb{E}\underbrace{\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}}_{\Lambda_{3,2}}

\displaystyle-~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\underbrace{\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}}_{\Lambda_{3,3}}-\mathbb{E}\underbrace{\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{\Lambda_{3,4}}.\vskip 2.84526pt

Case
(s,s)
:
i=\pi^{\natural}(i)
and
j=\pi^{\natural}(j)
.

First we compute
\Lambda_{3,1}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{3,1}=~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}}_{\mathbb{E}{\left\|\mathbf{X}_{i}\right\|}_{2}^{4}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)^{2}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}}_{\mathbb{E}{\left\|\mathbf{X}_{i}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}}_{\mathbb{E}{\left\|\mathbf{X}_{i}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)^{2}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}}_{\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)^{2}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\left(p+4\right)\left(p+8\right)\left[\left(\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\right)^{2}+2\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M})\right]+2\left(\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\right)^{2}+(p+6)\operatorname{Tr}\left(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}\right).\vskip 2.84526pt

Then, we consider
\Lambda_{3,2}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{3,2}=~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}}_{\mathbb{E}{\left\|\mathbf{X}_{i}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)^{2}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}}_{(p+2)\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}}_{(p+2)\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)^{2}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}}_{\mathbb{E}{\left\|\mathbf{X}_{j}\right\|}_{2}^{4}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\left(3p+8\right)\left[\left(\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\right)^{2}+\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M})+\operatorname{Tr}\left(\mathbf{M}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\right)\right]+(p+2)(p+4)\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}).\vskip 2.84526pt

In addition, we can verify that

\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{3,3}
and
\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{3,4}
are both
zero. Hence we conclude

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{3}=\left(p^{2}+15p+42\right)\left[\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\right]^{2}+\left(3p^{2}+37p+94\right)\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}).

(37)

Case
(s,d)
:
i=\pi^{\natural}(i)
and
j\neq\pi^{\natural}(j)
.
We can compute
\Lambda_{3,1}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{3,1}=~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}}_{\mathbb{E}{\left\|\mathbf{X}_{i}\right\|}_{2}^{4}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)^{2}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(j)}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}}_{0}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural-1}(j)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}}_{0}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(j)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}}_{0}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(j)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(j)}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}}_{\mathbb{E}{\left\|\mathbf{X}_{i}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(j)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural-1}(j)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}}_{\mathbbm{1}_{j=\pi^{\natural 2}(j)}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(j)}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{\mathbbm{1}_{i=\pi^{\natural}(j)}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\right]^{2}}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural-1}(j)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{0}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{0}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{0}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(j)}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{0}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural-1}(j)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{\mathbbm{1}_{j=\pi^{\natural 3}(i)}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\right]^{2}}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(j)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{\mathbbm{1}_{i=\pi^{\natural}(j)}p\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\right]^{2}}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(j)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{0}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(j)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(j)}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{0}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(j)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural-1}(j)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{0}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural-1}(j)}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{0}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural-1}(j)}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{\mathbbm{1}_{j=\pi^{\natural 3}(i)}\mathbb{E}{\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural-1}(j)}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(j)}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{0}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural-1}(j)}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural-1}(j)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{0}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\left[(p+1)\mathbbm{1}_{i=\pi^{\natural}(j)}+\mathbbm{1}_{j=\pi^{\natural 3}(i)}\right][\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})]^{2}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\left[2(p+1)\mathbbm{1}_{i=\pi^{\natural}(j)}+(p+4)\mathbbm{1}_{j=\pi^{\natural 3}(i)}\right]\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}).

(43)

Combing (
B.2
),
(
B.2
), (
B.2
),
and (
B.2
) together then yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{3}=\frac{4hp(p+2)}{n^{2}}\left[1+o(1)\right]\left[\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\right]^{2}+2p^{2}\left[1+o(1)\right]\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}).

(44)

The proof is thus completed by summarizing the computations thereof.
∎

Lemma 5
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{2}^{2}=2\left[(p+2)(p+3)+(n-2)(p+1)\right]{\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}=2np\left(1+p/n+o(1)\right){\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2},\vskip 2.84526pt

where
\Xi_{2}
is defined in (
20
).

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{2}^{2}=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\mathbf{W}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{W}\mathbf{B}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\right]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\operatorname{Tr}\left[\mathbf{X}\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\right]\mathbf{B}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\right]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\left\|\mathbf{X}\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)\right\|}_{2}^{2}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\mathbf{B}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\right]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\left\|\mathbf{X}\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)\right\|}_{2}^{2}\times{\left\|\mathbf{B}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\right].\vskip 2.84526pt

For the conciseness of notation, we assume
\pi^{\natural}(i)=1
and
j=2
w.l.o.g. Decomposing the term
{\left\|\mathbf{X}\left(\mathbf{X}_{1}-\mathbf{X}_{2}\right)\right\|}_{2}^{2}

we then separately bound the above three terms.
For the first term
\mathbb{E}{{\cal T}_{1}{\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{X}_{1}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}{{\cal T}_{1}{\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{X}_{1}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}}=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\left\|\mathbf{X}_{1}\right\|}_{2}^{4}+\left(\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{2}\right)^{2}\right){\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{X}_{1}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}{\left\|\mathbf{X}_{1}\right\|}_{2}^{4}{\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{X}_{1}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}}_{(p+2)(p+4){\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{2}\right)^{2}{\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{X}_{1}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}}_{(p+2){\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\small{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\textcircled{\raisebox{-.8pt}{1}}}}}}{{=}}(p+2)(p+5){\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2},

(45)

where
\raisebox{-.8pt}{1}⃝
is due to (
67
) and (
68
).

Similarly, for term
\mathbb{E}{{\cal T}_{2}{\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{X}_{1}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}}
, we
invoke (
67
) and (
68
),
which gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}{{\cal T}_{2}{\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{X}_{1}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}}=~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{2}\right)^{2}{\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{X}_{1}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}\right]}_{(p+2){\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}{\left\|\mathbf{X}_{2}\right\|}_{2}^{4}\times\mathbb{E}{\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{X}_{1}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}}_{p(p+2){\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\small{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\textcircled{\raisebox{-.8pt}{2}}}}}}{{=}}~

\displaystyle(p+1)(p+2){\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2},

(47)

where

is due to (

).

For the last term

\displaystyle\sum_{i\geq 3}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{X}_{1}-\mathbf{X}_{2}\right)\right)^{2}{\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{X}_{1}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\sum_{i\geq 3}\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\left\|\mathbf{X}_{1}\right\|}_{2}^{2}+{\left\|\mathbf{X}_{2}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\right)\cdot{\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural\top}\mathbf{X}_{1}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle 2\sum_{i\geq 3}(p+1){\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}=2(n-2)(p+1){\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\kern-1.72218pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|\kern-1.72218pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}.

The proof is then completed by combining
(

), (

Lemma 6
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{3}^{2}=~

\displaystyle 2n^{2}\left[\frac{p}{n}+\left(1-\frac{h}{n}\right)^{2}+\frac{p^{2}}{n^{2}}+\frac{4p(n-h)^{2}}{n^{3}}+o(1)\right]\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}),\vskip 2.84526pt

where
\Xi_{3}
is defined in (
20
).

Proof.

To begin with, we decompose the term
\mathbb{E}\Xi_{3}^{2}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Xi_{3}^{2}=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\underbrace{\left[\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)^{\top}\ \bm{\Sigma}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Sigma}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)\right]}_{\triangleq\Lambda_{1}}+2\mathbb{E}\underbrace{\left[\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)^{\top}\ \bm{\Sigma}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)\right]}_{\triangleq\Lambda_{2}}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\underbrace{\left[\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)^{\top}\ \bm{\Delta}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}-\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)\right]}_{\triangleq\Lambda_{3}}.

(49)

Step I.

First we consider
\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{1}
, which can be written as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{1}=~

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}\operatorname{Tr}\left(\bm{\Sigma}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Sigma}^{\top}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\small{\raisebox{0.6pt}{\textcircled{\raisebox{-.8pt}{1}}}}}}{{=}}2n^{2}\left[\frac{p}{n}+\left(1-\frac{h}{n}\right)^{2}+o(1)\right]\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}),

(50)

where
\raisebox{-.8pt}{1}⃝
is due to Lemma
12
.

Step II.

Then we turn to
\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2}
, which can be written as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2}=~

\displaystyle\ (n-h)\mathbb{E}\underbrace{\left[\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\ \mathbf{M}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\right]}_{\Lambda_{2,1}}+\ (n-h)\mathbb{E}\underbrace{\left[\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\ \mathbf{M}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}\right]}_{\Lambda_{2,2}}

\displaystyle-~

\displaystyle(n-h)\mathbb{E}\underbrace{\left[{\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}^{\top}\ \mathbf{M}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}{\mathbf{X}_{j}}\right]}_{\Lambda_{2,3}}-(n-h)\mathbb{E}\underbrace{\left[{\mathbf{X}_{j}}^{\top}\ \mathbf{M}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}{\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}\right]}_{\Lambda_{2,4}}.\vskip 2.84526pt

Case
(s,s)
:
i=\pi^{\natural}(i)
and
j=\pi^{\natural}(j)
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,1}=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\ \mathbf{M}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}+\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\right)\mathbf{X}_{i}=\ (p+3)\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,2}=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\ \mathbf{M}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}+\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\right)\mathbf{X}_{j}=\ (p+3)\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}).\vskip 2.84526pt

In addition, we can verify that
\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,2}
and

\Lambda_{2,3}
are both zero,
which suggests that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2}=2(n-h)(p+3)\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}).

(51)

Case
(s,d)
:
i=\pi^{\natural}(i)
and
j\neq\pi^{\natural}(j)
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,1}=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\ \mathbf{M}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}+\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(j)}^{\top}+\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural-1}(j)}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\right)\mathbf{X}_{i}=\ (p+2)\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M});

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,2}=~

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}+\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(j)}^{\top}+\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural-1}(j)}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\right)\mathbf{X}_{j}=\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}).\vskip 2.84526pt

Moreover, we have both
\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,3}
and
\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,4}

be zero,
which suggests that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2}=(n-h)(p+3)\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}).

(52)

Case
(d,s)
:
i\neq\pi^{\natural}(i)
and
j=\pi^{\natural}(j)
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,1}=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\ \mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\right)=\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,2}=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}=(p+2)\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}).\vskip 2.84526pt

Similar as above, we can verify both

\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,3}
and
\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,4}
are zero,
which suggests that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2}=(n-h)(p+3)\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}).

(53)

Case
(d,d)
:
i\neq\pi^{\natural}(i)
and
j\neq\pi^{\natural}(j)
.

Different from the above three cases, we have

\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,1}
and
\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,2}
be zero
and focus on the calculation of
\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,3}
and

\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,4}
, which proceeds as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,3}=~

\displaystyle\ \underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}^{\top}\ \mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}\right]}_{p\mathbbm{1}_{i=j}{\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}^{\top}\ \mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}\right]}_{\mathbbm{1}_{j=\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}{\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}^{\top}\ \mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(j)}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}\right]}_{p\mathbbm{1}_{i=j}{\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}^{\top}\ \mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural-1}(j)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}\right]}_{\mathbbm{1}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)=\pi^{\natural-1}(j)}{\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle 2\left[p\mathbbm{1}_{i=j}+\mathbbm{1}_{j=\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}\right]\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M});

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2,4}=~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\ \mathbf{M}{\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}}{\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}\right]}_{\mathbbm{1}_{i=j}{\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{X}_{j}}^{\top}\ \mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}{\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}\right]}_{p\mathbbm{1}_{j=\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}{\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{X}_{j}}^{\top}\ \mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(j)}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}{\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}\right]}_{\mathbbm{1}_{i=j}{\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbf{X}_{j}}^{\top}\ \mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural-1}(j)}^{\top}{\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}\right]}_{p\mathbbm{1}_{j=\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}{\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}}

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle 2\left[p\mathbbm{1}_{j=\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}+\mathbbm{1}_{i=j}\right]\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}),\vskip 2.84526pt

which suggests that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2}=-2(n-h)(p+1)\left(\mathbbm{1}_{j=\pi^{\natural 2}(i)}+\mathbbm{1}_{i=j}\right)\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}).

(54)

Combing (
51
),
(
52
),
(
53
),
and (
54
), we conclude

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{2}=\frac{2p(n-h)^{2}}{n}\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\left[1+o(1)\right].

Step III.

Then we turn to the calculation of
\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{3}
. First we perform
the following decomposition

\displaystyle\Lambda_{3}=~

\displaystyle\ \underbrace{\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\ \bm{\Delta}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{\Lambda_{3,1}}+\underbrace{\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}}_{\Lambda_{3,2}}-\underbrace{\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}^{\top}\ \bm{\Delta}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}}_{\Lambda_{3,3}}-\underbrace{\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\Delta}\mathbf{M}\bm{\Delta}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural}(i)}}_{\Lambda_{3,4}}.\vskip 2.84526pt

Case
(s,s)
:
i=\pi^{\natural}(i)
and
j=\pi^{\natural}(j)
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{3,1}=~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\ \mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)}_{\mathbb{E}{\left\|\mathbf{X}_{i}\right\|}_{2}^{4}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\ \mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)}_{(p+2){\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}}

\displaystyle+~

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\ \mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)}_{(p+2){\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\ \mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{i}\right)}_{(p+2){\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}}=(p+2)(p+7)\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M});

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{3,2}=~

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}+\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\right)\mathbf{M}\left(\mathbf{X}_{i}\mathbf{X}_{i}^{\top}+\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\right)\mathbf{X}_{j}=(p+2)(p+7)\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M}).\vskip 2.84526pt

As for
\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{3,3}
and
\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{3,4}
, easily we can
verify that they are both zero and hence have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Lambda_{3}=2(p+2)(p+7)\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})=2p^{2}\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{M})\left[1+o(1)\right].

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural-1}(j)}\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{X}_{j}\mathbf{X}_{\pi^{\natural-1}(j)}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{j}\right)}_{(p+2){\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\kern-1.20552pt\left|\mathbf{B}^{\natural}\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|\kern-1.20552pt\right|}_{\textup{F}}^{2}}
